### 3.3 Types, ownership, operand schema

```
  iN (1 ≤ N ≤ 128)   ptr (width ptr_width)   f64   token   qreg[n]   qreg[*]   region[A,W]
  wire   wire[N]      linear values in structured code and interfaces
  reg[N]              a location (oracle bodies, lprocs); not linear
```

**Widths.** The bound 1 ≤ N ≤ 128 applies to the type `iN` only (a lifted
row's `.iN` suffix names a location width, §4.2). `wire[N]`,
`reg[N]` and `qreg[n]` have N, n ≥ 1 and `region[A,W]` has A ≥ 0, W ≥ 1,
with no upper bound (a `qreg[*]` register's width is a run-time value n
≥ 0, §3.3 below); a value of these types is a bit-vector of its
width in every semantics (§14.1), including interface bundles,
pseudo-locations, locations, cells, stack cells and frame slots.

**Linear SSA with implicit versioning** (structured code and CC region
calls): a `wire`/`wire[N]` value is produced once and consumed once;
mode `in` consumes and re-produces a successor of the same name;
`consume` consumes; `produce` creates; a name is not used after
`consume`. **Regions are threaded the same way.** **Indexed operand**
`w[i]` (literal i) borrows wire i for one instruction.

**Disjointness.** The wire operands of one instruction are pairwise
distinct; **every wire of the effective control context is distinct from
every target and from every consumed or produced operand** of an
instruction executed under it (so `qpred p; x p` is ill-formed); a
predicate wire is not modified between its push and its matching pop; a
`qif`/`qloop … while` selector is not modified inside its arms or body.
A region's interface bundles and regions are pairwise disjoint.
Distinctness is judged on the wires denoted: a bundle and an indexed
operand of it, or two indexed operands denoting the same wire, overlap
and are not distinct. [deps: ISA-3.3-P3, ISA-4.4]

**Per-opcode operand schema** (§4.8): every opcode has a tier set, a
fixed arity, operand kinds and a result type; results are written first
(`w = op a b`), modes are never written, and **result types that are not
determined by the operands are written as a suffix** (`r = const.i32 5`,
`r = zext.i64 a`, `r = trunc.i8 a`, `r = ptrtoint.i64 p`, `p = inttoptr
a`). Width rules: `binop`: `iN` operands of equal width; `icmp`: `iN` or
`ptr` operands of equal width and the same type (CC/R; lifted forms:
§4.2); `select` arms equal width; `zext`/`sext` strictly widen, `trunc`
strictly narrows; `load.iN`
and `lift.load.iN` require N ∈ {8, 16, …, 128}; `measure` of `qreg[n]`
with n ≤ 128 yields `in`, and `measure.iN` (1 ≤ N ≤ 128) of a `qreg[*]`
yields `iN`; signed division truncates toward zero. The
literal c of `const.iN c` and `lift.const.iN c` satisfies −2^(N−1) ≤ c <
2^N and denotes c mod 2^N (both the signed and the unsigned spelling of
every value are accepted); any other literal is ill-formed (suite F).

**Lifted width rules** (suite F; a mismatch is ill-formed, never a
truncation or an extension). Every address operand (`a` of `lift.load`/
`lift.store`, `p` of `lift.boundscheck`/`lift.free`) and every address
result (`w` of `lift.addr`/`lift.alloc`) is `reg[ptr_width]`; the length
`n` of `lift.alloc`/`lift.boundscheck` is a location of any width or a
literal in [0, 2^ptr_width); `inrange` and every prospective sum are
computed at width max(widths of the operands involved) + 1 with no wrap.
The destination of `lift.label` and the operand of `qjumpr` have any
width N with 2^N > n (the index-space size). `lift.call` location
arguments have exactly the callee's parameter widths and its destination the callee's
result width; its token, classical and region lists match the callee's
corresponding groups in count, order and type (§3.7); `lift.ret v` has
the containing lproc's result width;
`lift.oracle_call`/`lift.oracle_uncall`/`lift.perm_call` operands have
# Mixed fat object — shared contract scope

Proposal for every revision in this directory; not a published ISA amendment.
Clause references are to [ISA 0.9](../../../spec/Bennett-ISA-Spec-0.9.md),
[the North Star](../../../../bennett/docs/Bennett-NorthStar.md), and, where
qualified, the maintained dialect 0.2/0.3 texts. This is the shared statement
required by bennett-4ht and the council rubric's standing mixed-fat-object check.

## Citable scoping statement

> A mixed fat object retains an ordinary native host binary and embeds ISA
> objects for marked device modules and their linked device closures (North
> Star §§2, 3.3, 4.4). ISA §§9.1–9.3 identify each device object and selected
> root; §12.3 certificates, owner records and reports cover that closure,
> with a separate binding/context set per device root and [] relative to
> that root (§12.1). Native entry, argv/envp and host-only libc need no ISA
> region, frame or certificate record; native comparison binds the selected
> materialised closure, not the whole host. Today §10 launches a device CC
> entry with copied arguments/entropy; §5.4 budgets bind device invocations,
> §3.3/§10 handles belong to a loaded program/run, and §7.3/§10 tapes cover
> device R effects. These require explicit container-to-object/root/certificate
> association, host-entry-to-device-entry bindings, budget/handle lifetime
> negotiation and tape/run association in a versioned mixed-launch contract.
> ISA §7.1 defines device memory but §10 defines no general host-memory-by-reference
> launch ABI: that contract must add address/layout mapping, range/liveness,
> aliasing, ownership/synchronisation, writeback and reversal rules, preserving
> dialect §§6.6, 8.5, 19.4–19.5. Until published, these are explicit
> bennett-4ht boundary obligations, not grounds to require whole-binary
> lowering or omit mixed execution.

## Present assumptions and exact publication changes

| Contract object | Assumption today | Exact change needed for mixed use |
|---|---|---|
| Object and linking (§§9.1–9.3) | One ISA object's closed sections and linked symbols; no native-container schema. | Publish a versioned inventory associating embedded/linked ISA object bytes and object_hash with selected device roots and certificates. A native-container hash, if used, is a separate identity. |
| Certificates/inventory (§§12.1–12.3) | Closure-keyed device-region records; current text does not explicitly delimit native inventory. | Insert the shared scoping rule in §12.3, cross-reference from §9.2, and require complete device-owner/link-proof coverage per root. Multiple device roots can share static closures without merging root-relative contexts. |
| Launch/entry (§10) | bennett_run(program, cc_entry, args, entropy) starts device CC; it is not native main. | Publish a host call/return shim and container/root selection/feature negotiation. Define native argument/result marshalling, including ptr (whose host binding §10 does not specify), status/fault propagation, suspend/reverse/close and ownership. Do not invent a current general launch shim. |
| Budgets (§5.4; §§10–11) | Validated token tuples and identity scoped to a program/run; action_call has no primary token (§4.7). | Bind selected device invocation budgets and token equality to the launch request; charge actual device/replay work. State host/shim accounting separately; a native process has no invented global B. |
| Handles (§3.3; §10; dialect §6.6) | Register ids refer to the loaded program's ledger; run ownership and token validity are explicit. | Map host-visible opaque handles to that program/ledger with validity and cross-launch ownership checks; preserve class vectors and token identities. Do not serialize native pointers as device ids or token tuples as identities. |
| Effect tape (§7.3; §10) | Run-associated R effects and explicit copied hostcall requests; no implicit log of native host I/O. | Associate each device run and tape with the launched root and define host effect/proxy ordering and replay at the boundary, preserving §7.4 reversal. Saved-journal interchange needs its own versioned replay profile. |
| Host memory by reference (§§7.1–7.2; dialect §§8.5, 17.1, 19.4–19.5) | Device layout plus dialect snapshot/replay and boundary-address/promotion rules; copied hostcall bytes do not provide general shared pointers. | Publish the mapping and lifetime protocol named in the statement, including allocator metadata/history and pointer-rich layouts, so ordinary host memory can be supplied safely without universal host promotion. Retain existing snapshot/replay as a construction, not the sole eventual capability. |

For example native main can call marked roots A and B in distinct ISA objects.
Each has its own object/root certificate and root context []; neither
certificate inventories main or host-only libc. A shared linked device
callee is checked in each root's actual invocation tree. A native comparison
executes the corresponding materialised region/callee closure under its
recorded layout, input and tool configuration (dialect §§17.7, 19.5, 19.9).

These notes specify boundary scope and exact remaining contract work; they
implement no container, host shim or memory registration. The existing
bennett-4ht issue tracks that publication/implementation obligation. Current
device contracts and their complete estimate/proof requirements remain in
force; an unimplemented boundary is not evidence that mixed execution works.

   103	normative insertion, including its tables. No unspecified field or
   104	inferred default is part of it.
   105	
   106	### 8.5 Imported models, parameters and hierarchical estimates (proposed normative text)
   107	
   108	An import MAY retain the legacy form `{version:string,map:object}`. Such
   109	an entry has its existing scalar import meaning; no QEC selection,
   110	hierarchy, physical-error guarantee or new parameter is inferred from it.
   111	An entry using this section has exactly `{version,map,model}`. `version`
   112	is the nonempty imported-model version, not the ISA sub-version.
   113	`model.schema` MUST equal `"bennett.import-model/1"`. All fields shown
   114	below are required unless suffixed `?`; absent optional arrays/maps are
   115	empty. Duplicate JSON keys, unknown fields, and unknown schema versions
   116	are invalid model encodings, rather than instructions to ignore data.
   117	
   118	**Scalar and unit types.** In the following schemas `S` is a nonempty
   119	Unicode string compared bytewise, `H` is exactly 64 lowercase hexadecimal
   120	digits, `Hex` is an even-length lowercase hexadecimal string (possibly
   121	empty), and `Q` is a reduced rational encoded as a string: `0` or a
   122	canonical signed decimal numerator, optionally followed by `/` and a
   123	positive canonical denominator greater than one. No leading `+`, leading
   124	zero, `-0`, denominator one, or reducible fraction is permitted. `Z` is
   125	the integral subset of Q, `N` the nonnegative integers, and `P` the strictly
   126	positive rationals. Mathematical integer ranges are unbounded; an implementation
   127	resource limit is an explicit unfinished-operation diagnostic, not a
   128	model-domain restriction or an executed program fault.
   129	
   130	A `Unit` is `{dimension:{S:Q},scale:P}`. Its dimension map omits zero
   131	exponents. Axis `s` denotes seconds; `physical_qubit` and `logical_qubit`
   132	are distinct axes. Other axes MUST include their source format and version in the axis
   133	identifier, unless an explicit dimension-preserving conversion is present in the normalized
   134	formula. An axis spelling itself is its identity; there is no implicit
   135	unit-equivalence registry.
   136	Equal labels alone imply no equivalence. A numeric value x in this unit
   137	has base value x*scale and the given dimension vector. Fractional
   138	exponents are admitted. Dimensionless units have `{}`. Unit metadata is
   139	not an approximation or an instruction to sum quantities.
   140	
   141	A target resource participating in a typed map MUST declare
   142	`dimension:{S:Q}` and `scale:P` in addition to its §8.2 `unit:string`.
   143	Its `unit` remains the display label. These two fields are optional and
   144	have no effect for resources not participating in this section. The
   145	mandatory `time` dimension is `{s:1}`, scale 1. Ordinary logical peak is
   146	`{logical_qubit:1}` and logical occupancy is
   147	`{logical_qubit:1,s:1}`; physical model outputs retain physical axes.
   148	A resource of kind `model` MUST supply this metadata; it has no automatic
   149	§8.2 scheduled-event aggregation and is reported in `model_estimates`.
   150	The required scheduled `time`, `peak_qubits` and `qubit_second` accounting
   151	remains in force. An objective that references a model resource MUST supply an explicit
   152	selector in the new optional `objective.model_outputs` map:
   153	
   154	```text
   155	objective = {weighted:{S:number},model_outputs?:{S:Selector}}
   156	          | {lexicographic:[S,...],model_outputs?:{S:Selector}}
   157	Selector = {model:S,node:S,metric:"duration"|"counts"|"occupancy"|"peak"|"quantity",
   158	            pool?:S,resource?:S,quantity?:S}
   159	```
   160	
   161	Each model-resource objective term has exactly one selector keyed by that
   162	resource name; other selector keys are invalid. A missing required selector
   163	is a model validation error at objective.model_outputs.<resource>, not a
   164	symbolic or unresolved expression. Model/node name the
   165	hierarchy evaluated using the resolved target and that objective term's
   166	explicit ModelInput below; no analytical report implicitly supplies inputs. Duration has seconds units and no optional selector field;
   167	counts requires resource, quantity requires its local quantity name, and
   168	occupancy/peak require pool; other optional selector fields are forbidden.
   169	Quantity selects that model output and converts through its map, counts
   170	selects its aggregate count, and pool metrics
   171	select the model-qualified pool. The selected dimensions must agree with
   172	the objective resource's and the value is converted to its scale. Existing
   173	weighted/lexicographic rules then apply to that scalar. An unbound
   174	objective is retained symbolically and has no silently assigned scalar
   175	value or completed concrete optimization verdict.
   176	
   177	**Objective input binding (§11 extension).** The scheduling request and
   178	schedule.bindings gain optional `model_inputs:{S:ModelInput}`. When there
   179	are model-resource objective terms, its keys MUST equal exactly their
   180	resource names; with none, omit it or use {}. Each entry is:
   181	
   182	```text
   183	ModelInput = {
   184	  root:{model:S,node:S},
   185	  models:{S:{model_hash:H,parameters:{S:ClosedExpr|null}}},
   186	  shared:[Shared,...]
   187	}
   188	```
   189	
   190	The root MUST equal the term's selector model/node. The models map lists
   191	exactly the transitively referenced models, their model_hash and every
   192	parameter once, in its declared unit. Parameter resolution, domain checks,
   193	operational freezing, invoke bindings and Shared use exactly the report
   194	rules below. This whole entry, including root, parameter expressions and
   195	reservations, is copied/validated by the request and serialized in
   196	schedule.bindings; it participates in schedule_hash. ModelInput supplies
   197	only classical model data and never coherent input values. A missing
   198	required entry gives an explicit incomplete-model-objective diagnostic,
   199	with no completed concrete schedule or optimization verdict. A present
   200	well-formed input with free dependent parameters retains a symbolic
   201	objective. The launch-estimate path carries the same ModelInput map under
   202	the corresponding §10.1 insertion; defaults never supply omitted model
   203	parameters or reservations. A malformed root/model/hash/selector/parameter/reservation is
   204	an explicit validation error, not an arbitrary choice of another report.
   205	Only a complete concrete input can support a completed concrete objective.
   206	
   207	A report used to certify an objective MUST bind the same device subject
   208	and context and reproduce that entry's root, models and shared values
   209	byte-for-byte after JCS. Other analytical reports may coexist and cannot
   210	retarget the objective. Changing only report outputs cannot change the
   211	schedule-bound objective input. Different legal shared reservations or
   212	algorithm bindings create different schedule identities even under one
   213	target_hash. A template with unresolved operational bindings still needs
   214	a newly resolved target before concrete scheduling, as specified below.
   215	
   216	Add this symbol in the new sched 0.10 ABI; JCS bytes are copied and
   217	validated, with the ordinary §11 ownership/status convention:
   218	
   219	```c
   220	bennett_status bennett_sched_request_set_model_inputs(
   221	    bennett_sched_request_t*, const uint8_t* model_inputs_jcs, size_t len);
   222	```
   223	
   603	
   604	### Corresponding §§10–10.1 insertion (proposed normative text)
   605	
   606	Publish this additive symbol in **launch 0.10**, using the existing
   607	opaque types, status and ownership conventions. It is not an amendment
   608	to the launch 0.9 symbol, object layout or target/certificate schema:
   609	
   610	```c
   611	bennett_status bennett_estimate_run_with_model_inputs(
   612	    bennett_program_t*, const char* region, bennett_token,
   613	    const char* variant, uint32_t inherited_controls,
   614	    const uint8_t* params_jcs, size_t params_len,
   615	    const uint8_t* model_inputs_jcs, size_t model_inputs_len,
   616	    bennett_estimate_t** out);
   617	```
   618	
   619	Add to §10.1 Resolved request:
   620	
   621	```text
   622	A launch 0.10 estimate request additionally carries model_inputs JCS bytes
   623	governed by §8.5. The implementation MUST copy and validate them before
   624	returning an estimate; length is the UTF-8 byte length excluding a NUL.
   625	This is the model_inputs map itself, not a hash, a report, a file path,
   626	or an addition to the signature-order params_jcs array. Subsequent changes
   627	to the caller's buffer MUST NOT retarget the copied request/estimate.
   628	Duplicate/unknown keys and malformed ModelInputs are validation errors.
   629	
   630	A default-only effective schedule MUST serialize exactly those validated
   631	inputs in bindings.model_inputs, after JCS canonicalization; they
   632	participate in schedule_hash. The explicit empty-entries default schedule
   633	and every existing object/interface/closure/target/root/variant/control/
   634	accuracy/invocation/expansion binding remain required.
   635	
   636	An embedded schedule is applicable only when every existing §5.4/§11
   637	binding matches AND its ModelInputs match the request byte-for-byte after
   638	JCS. An embedded schedule with different model parameters, model identity,
   639	root or shared reservations is inapplicable to this request. Use the §11
   640	default realisation with the requested inputs; do not borrow the embedded
   641	schedule's inputs, silently substitute a report, or reject the complete
   642	request merely because that embedded schedule does not match.
   643	
   644	Every complete, well-typed resolved request, including its required
   645	ModelInputs, MUST be supported. This replaces the inherited completeness
   646	sentence for launch 0.10 model-resource requests: complete means the
   647	ordinary resolved classical arguments and valid budget plus the complete
   648	concrete objective inputs and operational target required by §8.5.
   649	Missing required ModelInputs produce incomplete-model-objective before an
   650	estimate is returned; no parameter or reservation is inferred. This is
   651	E_ARG with that diagnostic and no estimate object, not an ISA fault, a
   652	program-language restriction or E_SCHEDULE_INFEASIBLE. A present, valid
   653	input with free dependent parameters retains §8.5's symbolic objective;
   654	it cannot claim a completed concrete estimate/optimization. A malformed
   655	selector remains a validation error, distinct from missing/free input.
   656	
   657	For a target without model-resource objective terms, an omitted input map
   658	or {} denotes the empty map and retains the existing complete estimate
   659	path. A null pointer with zero model_inputs_len denotes omission; a null
   660	pointer with nonzero length is E_ARG. A supplied nonempty byte string
   661	MUST encode the map; a zero-length nonnull buffer is also omission.
   662	The 0.10 symbol without model-input arguments is equivalent to omission,
   663	and thus cannot infer inputs required by a model-resource objective.
   664	The ordinary scalar launch 0.9 API and all its required demo estimates
   665	remain unchanged. A model-input error leaves the loaded program and
   666	independently valid execution/estimate paths available.
   667	```
   668	
   669	Add to §10.1 Certificate binding:
   670	
   671	```text
   672	The effective schedule's bindings.model_inputs MUST reproduce the request's
   673	validated canonical input map. A certifying model report MUST reproduce
   674	its corresponding entry under §8.5 and match the actual device subject
   675	and context. A checker independently validates the supplied inputs,
   676	rebuilds the bound realisation and verifies both the existing scheduled
   677	§8.2 rows and the selected model objective. The two accounting views
   678	remain separate; model-only outputs add no estimate_get scalar row.
   679	Changing only a non-operational algorithm binding or shared reservation
   680	can change schedule_hash and certificate bytes without changing target_hash.
   681	```
   682	
   683	The explicit transport is the selected resolution of R2-1; no default-only
   684	exemption from ModelInput binding is introduced. The versioned C interface
   685	and publication schemas must include this contract before claiming 0.10
   686	consumer support. It defines an estimate of a selected device invocation;
   687	in a mixed fat object it does not make native main an ISA entry or supply
   688	a general host-memory-by-reference ABI.
   689	
   690	### Corresponding §12.3 insertion (proposed normative text)
   691	
   692	A region MAY carry `model_estimates` as specified in §8.5. Those reports
   693	are separate, explicitly identified accounting views. They MUST NOT be
   694	added to the region's required §8.2 scheduled-leaf resources, charged again
   695	as parent-plus-child costs, or substituted for the region's logical error
   696	or fault-rate obligations. An invocation's selected QEC distance, hardware binding and every other
   697	operational dependency affect target_hash; analytical non-operational
   698	algorithm inputs and hierarchy reports affect certificate bytes, and
   699	objective ModelInputs additionally affect schedule_hash. Physical failure charges and all
   700	physical-model assumptions remain separate from epsilon and correctness
   701	claims. No model output changes the object's instruction semantics.
   702	
   703	## Fat-object applicability
   704	
   705	Incorporate [the shared scoping statement](../2026-11-02-rev2/fat-object-scope.md#citable-scoping-statement).
   706	Append to §12.3, in the new version:
   707	
   708	```text
   709	In a mixed fat object, model_estimates attach to the certificate of the
   710	identified embedded/linked device ISA object and selected device-root
   711	closure. The containing certificate's object_hash and closure_hash name
   712	that device object and root, not the enclosing native binary. The fat
   713	inventory associates each device object/root with its certificate and
   714	target-bound reports; multiple roots retain independent contexts and
   715	objective inputs. Native main, argv/envp and host-only libc remain native
   716	and need no device resource report or certificate record. Native
   717	comparison binds the corresponding materialised outlined-region/callee
   718	closure and recorded configuration. This report asserts no whole-native-
   719	program resource total. Budgets, handles, tapes and host memory follow
   720	the device scope and explicit remaining launch obligations in the shared
   721	statement; this extension implements no host shim.
   722	```
   723	
   724	Example: native main performs host-only work then calls marked module M
   725	through a device CC wrapper. M's object/root certificate carries its
   726	surface:algorithm report. Main and host-only libc acquire no model_estimates;
   727	a second device root obtains its own context/input/certificate binding.
   728	General host-memory-by-reference launch mapping remains explicit
   729	bennett-4ht contract work, not an assumption supplied by this report.
   730
